市場焦點
美國
• 9月CPI年增3.7%、PPI年增6.3%，雙雙高於前期 ，能源價格的急升是通膨回升的主要推力 。
雖然核心CPI年增率僅升至2.6%，但住房與服務價格仍維持黏著，使通膨降溫過程出現反覆；
同時PPI增幅明顯高於 CPI，反映企業成本壓力偏高 ，未來不排除進一步向終端價格傳導 。整
體而言，CPI與PPI同步回升反映通膨降溫進程仍存在不確定性 ，進而令市場重估通膨回落速
度與對Fed貨幣政策路徑的審慎預期。
AI
• AI雲端業者 CoreWeave的5年期CDS利差一度突破 1,140個基點 ，旗下兩檔債券殖利率逼近
15%，相關賣壓並進一步蔓延至股票市場 。全球最大半導體設備商ASML表示，客戶已開始洽
談2029至2030年的訂單；晶圓代工龍頭台積電財報亦優於市場預期 。與此同時，台灣出口增

速創3月以來新高，韓國9月半導體出口年增逾260%，顯示半導體需求持續強勁，預期第三季
相關企業獲利仍有望維持強勁。
中國
• 9月通膨加速主要源於中東能源衝擊及 AI硬體需求，能源和科技領域之外 ，普遍性的通縮壓力
仍在，彭博表示以GDP平減指數衡量 ，經歷三年下滑後 ，整體經濟預計將在 2026年走出通縮 ，
投資上短期可聚焦，受惠AI資本支出與能源價格上行的半導體、伺服器及能源相關族群。

美股獲利引擎轉向半導體/核心AI與能源
• 雖然標普500指數在2026年第三季展現出獲利韌性 ，但隨著油價上漲侵蝕利潤率 、消費者受通膨壓抑 ，以及分析師下調未
來預測動能，市場未來的容錯空間正在縮小。
• AI與半導體仍為獲利領頭羊：科技與半導體持續為標普500指數獲利成長的核心動力，其中約20家企業組成的核心AI一籃子
股票(權重約34.6%)對指數獲利的貢獻度已超過科技七雄(Mag 7)，且此優勢預計將至少延續至2028年底。
• 油價推升能源產業，接棒成為強棒：受到近期國際油價上揚影響，能源產業獲利預測顯著上修，預計獲利成長勁道將延續至
2027年初。在接下來的三個季度中，能源板塊的收益成長表現預計將超越科技七雄。
• 消費與運輸業面臨成本壓頂：相較於科技與能源的強勁，必需消費品與交通運輸業正承受顯著壓力。例如，達美航空受高燃

料成本拖累，而Nike與百事可樂則反映出消費者因持續通膨而縮減支出的困境。
7月以來EPS和營收成長預期修正
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.16
剔除能源、科技七雄和AI一籃子股票後的指數收益成長

顧
利潤率高檔盤整、關稅退款帶來短補，但動能已見放緩
• 利潤率高檔盤整，淨利潤率提升受限：標普500成分股第二季營運利潤率(18.7%)與淨利潤率(15.6%)均創下新高。雖然第三
季營運利潤率預估可進一步提升至19.4%，但淨利潤率預計微幅上升至15.8%，顯示整體企業淨利潤的擴張動能正漸趨平緩 。
• 700億美元關稅退款提供短線補藥： 受惠於美國政府依據國際緊急經濟權力法 (IEEPA)的追償退稅，估計仍有至少700億美元
的關稅款將退還給標普 500成分股(主要集中於工業、非必需消費及醫療保健等 )，這預計能為2026年標普500指數每股收益
(EPS)成長帶來約235個基點、利潤率提升約34個基點的短期提振。
• 上修動能開始轉弱：雖然整體財報指引動量仍維持正值，但過去六週內，分析師對未來12個月EPS與收入的修正動能出現頻

繁下修，這意味著市場先前的極度樂觀情緒正在消退，未來財報季度要優於預期的門檻正在提高。
• 小結與投資建議： 標普500指數短期內靠著半導體 /AI與能源雙引擎拉抬 ，加上關稅退款的一次性利多注入 ，下半年整體獲
利依然具備支撐。然而，高油價帶來的營運成本上升 ，以及終端消費力道的疲軟 ，正逐漸侵蝕非科技/非能源企業的利潤 ，
而大盤廣度不足(僅少數板塊支撐獲利)，代表未來投資風險正逐漸集中 ，但AI算力需求仍屬硬性需求，但波動加劇下，投資
建議以定期定額方式投資美股核心AI與半導體龍頭股票。
利潤率預測
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.16
各產業第二季度收到的關稅退款
未來12月EPS預期修正動能與營收

顧
美國9月CPI、PPI 同步升溫，能源衝擊擴大通膨與利率風險
• 能源價格急升，消費通膨再升溫：美國9月CPI月增0.6%、
年增3.7%，高於前月 3.4%，其中汽油月增 9.1%、能源
價格月增 5.2%，汽油單項即貢獻整體 CPI當月漲幅逾半；
另一方面，核心CPI月增0.3%、年增2.6%、住房指數月
增0.4%，顯示能源雖是本次通膨回升主因 ，但住房及部
分服務價格仍具黏著性，令通膨降溫進程面臨反覆。
• 上游價格大幅攀升 ，成本傳導風險升高： 9月PPI最終需
求月增1.3%、年增6.3%，創2022年3月以來最大增幅，
其中最終需求商品月增 2.6%，能源價格大漲 10.8%，汽
油價格更跳升 20.3%；核心PPI月增0.5%、年增5.0%，
顯示價格壓力不僅來自能源 ，也涉及部分商品與服務環

節；後續是否進一步推升至 CPI，仍須觀察企業成本轉
嫁程度及需求變化。
• 市場看法： 通膨回升恐提高 Fed再度升息機率 ，並延長
高利率維持時間 ，甚至不排除進一步緊縮的可能 ，短期
有利支撐美債殖利率及美元表現 ，不過股、債市後續走
勢仍將受到貨幣政策預期與風險情緒變化影響 。資產配
置建議可關注具成本轉嫁能力 、現金流穩健的能源類股；
債券則宜以短中天期 、高信用評等標的為主 ，以降低利
率敏感度。黃金可作為分散地緣政治及通膨風險的輔助
配置，但不宜將任何單一資產視為必然受惠標的。
CPI 與核心 CPI 走勢變動

顧
供給面推動中國9月CPI升0.9%
• 供給面推動中國9月通膨復甦：中國9月通膨數據顯示，CPI年增率由8月的0.8%升至0.9%，連續兩個月擴大，但低於市場預期
的1.0%，物價上漲主要受到能源及旅遊價格帶動，而食品價格跌幅進一步擴大，使整體通膨壓力仍相對溫和，扣除食品與能源
後，核心CPI維持在1.0%，反映內需需求尚未明顯升溫 。另一方面，PPI年增率由3.8%大幅升至5.0%，優於市場預期，且月增
率達1.2%，創4月以來最大漲幅，顯示上游原物料及工業品價格回升，須關注未來是否逐步傳導至消費端價格。
• 展望後市： 9月通膨加速主要源於中東能源衝擊及 AI硬體需求 ，能源和科技領域之外 ，普遍性的通縮壓力仍 在，彭博表示 以
GDP平減指數衡量，經歷三年下滑後，整體經濟預計將在2026年走出通縮，值得注意的是，這種供給面驅動的通膨復甦，將降

低官方推出需求刺激措施的緊迫感 ，而要反轉通縮循環正需要官方舉措，投資上短期可聚焦受惠AI資本支出與能源價格上行的
半導體、伺服器及能源相關族群。
資料來源：彭博，2026.10.14
通膨集中於能源和AI相關行業

顧
過去18個月來，新興市場股票表現亮眼，累積報酬率超過標普500指數
的兩倍
• 過去18個月來，新興市場股票表現亮眼，累積報酬率超過標普500指數的兩倍：主因為獲利帶動成長，整體而言，新興市場企
業獲利表現強勁 ，而且仍持續加速成長 ，高盛預估2026年新興市場企業盈餘成長率將達 55%，超過標普500企業盈餘成長速
度的兩倍，且新興市場股票估值較美國股市存在超過40%的折價空間，評價相對具吸引力，今年以來流入新興市場股市的資金
已接近500億美元，創下過去20年來最強勁紀錄之一。更重要的是新興市場國家目前貢獻了全球近 80%的AI硬體出口。隨著AI
產業持續發展，受惠範圍有望從少數龍頭企業擴大至更多國家及公司，特別是那些能夠協助解決供應鏈瓶頸的企業。
• 結論：後市來看在AI產業趨勢、相對低估的評價水準，以及強勁企業獲利成長三大因素支撐下，新興市場股票未來一年仍具備

進一步上漲的潛力，有機會延續目前的強勢表現。

AI投資支撐美國成長，融資需求升高使長端利率與信用風險並存
• AI基礎建設持續擴張 ，成為企業投資重要動能 ：大型雲端業者
持續投入資料中心 、半導體、網路設備與電力基礎設施 ，帶動
相關產業資本支出與供應鏈需求 。摩根大通資產管理指出 ，納
入資料中心開發商及半導體投資後，AI 基礎建設至 2030 年的
累計投資需求可能達約 5兆美元 ，顯示AI投資循環仍具規模 ，
但實際經濟貢獻與企業獲利 ，仍取決於投資效率及商業化成果 。
• 外部融資需求增加 ，債券供給與投資回報成關鍵： 隨AI投資規
模擴大，大型科技企業逐步增加公司債與外部融資 ，在企業債
與公債供給同步增加下 ，長天期利率面臨上行壓力 。若未來AI
獲利成長未能跟上資本支出擴張速度 ，可能壓縮自由現金流與

資本報酬率，進一步擴大信用利差 ，並提高高估值科技股的評
價修正風險
• 市場觀察：AI基礎建設仍是未來數年推動全球科技投資與企業
數位轉型的成長主軸，相關需求具備中長期發展空間，而2026
年市場已逐漸轉向檢視投資回報率 、現金流創造能力及融資結
構是否具備可持續性 ，因此單純依賴資本支出擴張已不足以支
撐所有AI概念股評價 。建議配置可兼顧具獲利與現金流支撐的
AI供應鏈，以及高信用評等 、中短天期債券；對長天期債券則
須評估供給與利率風險 ，並審慎檢視高負債 AI企業的償債能力
及投資回報。
資料來源：Goldman Sachs，永豐金證券， 2026.10.12

AI熱潮、高債務與網路威脅，IMF示警三大金融穩定風險升高
資料來源：Bloomberg Finance L.P， Dealogic，Zero day Clock and IMF staff calculations.， 2026.10.13
AI相關風險持續升溫 主權債券殖利率攀升 網路資安威脅升高
大型雲端科技企業債券發行規模
（占投資等級公司債市場比重) 10年期公債殖利率平均變動幅度 零時差漏洞占比（%）
• AI估值高度集中，市場重定價風險升溫：IMF於10月《全球金融穩定報告》指出，AI相關投資已成為全球成長的重要支撐，
但資金與漲勢高度集中於少數企業及市場，一旦獲利成長不如預期或估值遭重新檢視，可能引發科技股修正並透過風險偏好
下降擴散至整體金融市場。
• 通膨與財政疑慮恐推升債券市場波動： IMF認為，若能源價格推升通膨壓力或市場對財政永續性產生疑慮 ，主權債殖利率可

能進一步上升，進而提高全球融資成本與資產重估風險；在高利率與高債務環境下，金融條件快速緊縮的情況將明顯提高。
• 網路安全風險成為潛在威脅： 隨金融體系數位化程度提高 ，IMF將大型網路攻擊列為重要風險來源之一 。若攻擊事件波及支
付系統、金融基礎設施或雲端服務平台，不僅可能影響市場運作效率，也可能透過信心衝擊與流動性壓力擴大金融市場波動。
• 投資建議：市場成長動能仍受AI支撐，但在金融穩定風險上升情境下，建議採取「AI受惠股＋高品質債券＋防禦型資產」的
槓鈴策略；核心配置可聚焦全球AI基礎建設供應鏈及投資等級債，藉由企業長期獲利成長與債券避險功能，同時因應估值修
正及金融條件收緊風險。

AI隱憂浮現但動能仍在
• CoreWeave信用風險亮紅燈：市場憂心雲端業者在算力租賃業務產生足夠回報前，恐難以負擔龐大債務，導致信用風險升
溫。上週，AI雲端業者CoreWeave的5年期信用違約交換（CDS）利差一度突破1,140個基點，升至通常反映嚴重信用壓力
的水準；旗下兩檔債券殖利率逼近15%，其中2032年到期的無擔保債券於上週三跌破面值的80%，相關賣壓並進一步蔓延
至股票市場。
• AI半導體產業的基本面仍具韌性：全球最大半導體設備商艾司摩爾（ASML）表示，客戶已開始洽談2029至2030年的訂單；
晶圓代工龍頭台積電財報亦優於市場預期。與此同時，台灣出口增速創3月以來新高，韓國9月半導體出口年增逾260%，顯
示半導體需求持續強勁，預期第三季相關企業獲利仍有望維持強勁。

美銀美林調查指出，全球經理人對景氣與股市仍維持偏多立場
資料來源：BofA Global Fund Manager Survey，2026.10
• 經理人樂觀情緒維持高檔： 美銀美林10月全球基金經理人調查顯示 ，市場風險偏好仍高 ，平均現金水位維持在 3.9%的低檔，
科技股配置則升至2020年8月以來最高，反映經理人對AI產業及企業獲利前景仍具信心 。然而，整體經濟成長預期已轉趨保
守，淨4%的受訪者預期未來 12個月經濟成長放緩 。極端風險方面，債券殖利率無序攀升仍居首位 ，占比31%；AI泡沫及通
膨再起分別為23%及20%。此外，39%的經理人認為，大型雲端業者資本支出是系統性信用事件的主要潛在來源 ，顯示市場
焦點已從AI需求成長延伸至龐大資本支出的獲利回收能力。

• 美國公債殖利率走高 ，債券的收益吸引力逐漸回升： 調查顯示，37%的受訪者表示，當美國10年期公債殖利率升至 6%時，
才會考慮由股票轉向債券；另有19%認為目前殖利率已具吸引力，顯示部分經理人已開始重新評估固定收益資產的配置價值 。
不過，債券整體仍處淨減碼，且經理人對科技股的配置持續升高 ，反映市場尚未全面轉向防禦。後續仍須關注美債殖利率變
化、AI資本支出的投資回報，以及企業獲利能否支撐偏高的市場評價。
資料來源：BofA Global Fund Manager Survey，2026.10

台積電財報佳卻未激勵股價，短線面臨總經干擾
• 台積電Q3營收、獲利及EPS同步改寫單季新高：營收年增50.9%、季增17.6%，稅後純益7,630.6億元，年增68.7％、季增8
％；每股稅後純益(EPS)29.43元，整體優於市場預期，但毛利率66.4％較第二季的67.7％下降。
• 財測展望：第四季財測營收預估 523~543億美元，季增上看15％、年增65％，毛利率預估65%~67%，反映高階晶片需求
依然強勁。全年展望方面，預期2026年美元營收年增約44%，較上季預估的「略高於40%」說法更加明確。此外，重申全
年資本支出600~640億美元不變。
• 市場反應：台積電法說會隔日股價小幅下跌，法說會未出現激勵行情，主要原因包含美國通膨升溫壓力、外資連續調節部位，

以及毛利率略為下降。短線籌碼面可能仍會調整，但台積電董事長魏哲家在法說會中強調，第二波AI需求正強勁興起，台積
電正透過多種途徑全力擴充產能，顯示高階晶片前景明確，中長期具投資價值，多家投資機構也紛紛上調目標價。
資料來源：台積電法說會，兆豐銀行整理，2026.10.15。
法說會重點整理 說明
AI需求持續升溫
魏哲家坦言，目前仍難以判斷晶圓供需缺口將擴大或縮小，因為
客戶需求持續增加，台積電也正積極提高產能。儘管消費性電子
市場仍受到零組件漲價及總體經濟不確定性影響，但先進製程需
求依然強勁，公司預估2026年全年美元營收將年增約44％。
擴廠進度
(1)台南科學園區新建3奈米廠預計2027年下半年量產，並決定在
台南再增建一座3奈米廠，規畫2028年投入量產。
(2)美國亞利桑那州第二座晶圓廠已完成廠房興建，預計2027年
第三季量產，時程較原先規畫略為提前；日本第二座晶圓廠則將
導入3奈米製程，預計2028年量產。
台積電證實洽談Terafab
合作
魏哲家強調，目前相關合作仍在討論階段，尚未有結論。不過，

無論未來採取何種合作模式，晶圓廠都必須由台積電負責營運。
電力供應疑慮
美國電力供應情況是公司評估AI長期需求的重要因素之一，因此
台積電進一步確認當地電力供應狀況，目前相關研究及評估結果
均良好，尚未發現明顯問題。

• 全球經濟正處於「韌性仍在、風險升高」的階段：國際貨幣基金組織(IMF)指出，AI投資熱潮持續推動美國、台灣等國家成
長，但中東地緣政治衝突、高通膨風險、政府債務問題與AI泡沫化疑慮，也同時為市場埋下不確定因素。
• 市場永遠充滿雜音，定期定額紀律投資更具優勢：本許多人認為定期定額的核心在於「平均成本」，但更重要的是幫助投資
人克服人性。市場上漲時持續投入，可以參與企業獲利成長帶來的成果，市場下跌時持續扣款，則能以較低價格累積更多單
位數。換句話說，定期定額讓投資人不需要每天判斷：明天會不會跌？現在是不是太貴？要不要先停扣觀望？長期而言，紀

律投資往往比短期擇時的成效更佳。
• AI時代更適合定期定額：儘管IMF認為人工智慧發展為全球經濟成長帶來貢獻，但同時也警告，若市場對AI未來獲利能力過
度樂觀，不排除出現股市估值修正。根據歷史經驗，每一次市場重大修正往往也孕育下一輪成長契機，不論AI發展是否出現
泡沫，善用定期定額策略可以在機會與風險之間達到平衡。
韌性仍在、風險升溫，紀律投資更穩健
任一月份定期定額進場投資試算
註：以MSCI全球指數2010/1/31~2025/12/31試算。假設定期定額每月月底投入、
原幣計算。指數資料試算僅供參考，非投資商品之實際績效。資料來源Bloomberg。
短進短出錯失行情的代價大
註：以美國標普500指數為試算範例，期間為1980年~2025年。資料來源Bloomberg。
投資人無法直接投資指數，資訊僅供歷史統計說明，非預測投資商品之績效。

3. 基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益 ，投資人申購
前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 本資料所載內容如涉及商品資訊 ，商品相關資訊仍以經核可之條款及文宣 (DM)為主，本行網站已備有公開說明書可供查閱基金應負擔之費用及相關投資風險 。投
資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。
5. 本資料所載內容取自本公司相信可靠之資料來源，且為特定日期之判斷，本公司不保證其即時性、精確性及完整性，亦不提供或嘗試遊說客戶做為投資之判斷依據。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。
11. 屬基金資訊觀測站ESG基金專區所列之基金，有關基金之ESG資訊，投資人應於申購前詳閱基金公開說明書或投資人須知所載之基金所有特色或目標等資訊。

市場焦點
美國
• 9月綜合PMI升至58.4，創2021年7月以來新高 ，製造業與服務業同步加速 ，新訂單及就業亦
明顯成長 ，顯示消費 、AI投資與國防支出支撐景氣動能 。不過，需求快速擴張也加劇供應鏈
瓶頸，企業投入價格升至 2022年10月以來高點，燃料、運輸及原物料成本上升 ，顯示景氣韌
性與通膨壓力同步升溫，增加Fed政策維持緊縮的壓力。
美國
期中選舉
• 目前民調顯示 ，民主黨可能取得兩院或眾議院多數 ，這將有助於縮減未來財政赤字預期 ，進
而帶動10年期美債殖利率下滑 ，對長天期美債形成支撐 。此外既有資料顯示 ，選後一年美股
平均報酬率達12.4%，主因為政治不確定性降低，市場焦點重新回到經濟成長、企業獲利及貨
幣政策等基本面因素，進而帶動投資信心提升。

越南
• 越南股市正式納入富時羅素新興市場指數 。估計此次納入最高可引導 60億美元的資金重新流
入越南。資產管理公司Vanguard也計畫在未來幾年內，將對越南的投資額提升至約 25億美元。
• 越南股市短期仍面臨逆風 ，除了能源通膨壓力居高不下恐拖累經濟成長 ，今年以來越南成為
美國最大貿易逆差國，加上美方企圖阻止越南成為中國商品流入的中轉國 ，美對越的301條款
調查為越南出口額帶來更多不確定性。

美國景氣強勢擴張，通膨與利率壓力再升
• 企業活動創逾五年新高 ，製造業與服務業同步加速擴張 ：S&P
Global美國9月綜合PMI初值升至58.4，創2021年7月以來新高，
且連續四個月加速成長；服務業 PMI升至58.7（59個月高點）、
製造業PMI升至57.0（52個月高點），產出亦同步加速 ，加上
訂單與就業成長皆達近年最快 ，顯示內需、AI投資及國防支出
共同支撐企業活動。S&P Global依PMI調查推估經濟年成長約
5%，Q3則上看4%，反映短期景氣動能明顯增強。
• 供給受限推高成本 ，通膨壓力再升溫： 需求快速擴張同時推高
供應鏈與產能壓力 ，企業未完成訂單升至 2022年5月以來高點 ，
供應商交貨延遲亦明顯惡化 ，反映產能不足 ，投入價格指數升

至2022年10月以來新高 ，企業主要反映燃料 、運輸、原物料
及薪資成本上升 ，顯示供給受限與能源成本可能持續向終端價
格傳導，增加通膨黏著風險並提高Fed維持緊縮政策的壓力。
• 後市觀察：數據公布後，10年期美債殖利率一度升至約 5.12%，
創2007年以來新高 ，市場對10月再升息的押注亦升溫 ，短線
需關注供應鏈壓力是否緩解 ，以及後續ISM、就業與物價數據
能否確認通膨再升溫趨勢 。投資策略建議關注利率敏感度較低
的高品質成長股或防禦型資產 ，可適度布局受惠強勁內需與 AI
／國防支出的美股大型科技與工業類股 ，並留意升息環境下金
融股的利差受益機會 ，整體建議維持分散配置並嚴控利率風險 。
資料來源：S&P Global PMI, Bureau of Economic Analysis via S&P Global Market
Intelligence， 2026.9.24

能源成本升高推升通膨，利率維持高檔壓力升溫
• 能源與運輸成本上升，增加通膨黏著風險：中東衝突與
俄羅斯煉油設施遭攻擊 ，使全球柴油供應持續偏緊 ，燃
料價格及運輸成本同步上升；由於柴油廣泛應用於運輸 、
農業與製造業，價格上漲將透過物流及生產成本向下游
傳導。目前全球柴油庫存偏低 、煉油產能受限，若供應
中斷延續，可能使能源價格維持高檔並延長通膨壓力。
• 公債與企業債供給增加 ，長端利率壓力升高：美國財政
赤字及政府融資需求偏高 ，加上大型科技公司持續為 AI
資料中心等基礎建設籌資 ，債券供給增加使市場承接壓
力升高；在通膨預期未完全穩定的情況下 ，投資人對長
天期債券要求較高的期限溢酬 ，進一步推升長端殖利率 ，
同時AI相關企業債近期亦出現利差擴大 ，反映市場對大

量發債及資本支出回收期的關注升高。
• 資產配置面臨成長與通膨雙重拉扯： 能源價格、企業融
資成本及長端殖利率若持續處於高檔 ，將提高企業資金
成本並壓縮高估值資產的評價空間；同時 ，利率與能源
價格波動也可能擴大股 、債市震盪。因此資產配置建議
降低長天期債券對利率變化的敏感度 ，提高對通膨與利
率波動的防禦能力 ，並以現金流穩定、具備定價能力之
優質標的為核心。
資料來源：Federal Reserve Bank of St. Louis via FRED ， 2026.9.8

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.9

美國期中選舉-目前民調顯示民主黨可能取得兩院或眾議院多數
• 目前民調顯示民主黨可能取得兩院或眾議院多數 ：最新民調顯示，民主黨
於11月美國期中選舉，取得國會多數席次的機率已領先共和黨，根據市場
情境分析，期中選舉不影響既有法案執行 ，但將左右未來兩年的財政預算 、
稅制改革 、政府支出及國會監督權限 。情境(1)若共和黨續掌國會 ，減稅
政策延長、產業回流、關稅及移民限制等議程有望加速推進 ，財政擴張力
道可能進一步增強 。情境(2)分裂國會 ，兩黨制衡將使重大法案推動難度
提高，新增減稅與大型財政刺激方案較難落地 。情境(3)民主黨全面掌控
國會，則可能提高對政府政策的監督力度 ，政策方向偏向提高稅負與監管 ，
壓抑企業獲利 ，使政策環境趨向平衡。

美國期中選舉-對於股債市的影響
• 對股市的影響：美國銀行分析1900至2025年共31次美國期中選舉，資料發現標普500在期中選舉前12個月平均報酬約為2.9%，
低於所有年度平均 8.9%。因為市場在選舉前往往受到政策不確定性 、政黨競爭及投資人觀望情緒影響 ，波動相對較大 。然而
此現象不代表選舉必然導致市場疲弱，因為當時的經濟環境、通膨、利率及企業獲利等因素同樣扮演重要角色。期中選舉後12
個月的股市表現通常較佳 。歷史數據顯示，選後一年美股平均報酬率達12.4%。主因為政治不確定性降低，市場焦點重新回到
經濟成長、企業獲利及貨幣政策等基本面因素，進而帶動投資信心提升。
• 對債市的影響：若民主黨取得國會主導權，市場普遍預期財政支出擴張幅度將較受節制 ，有助於縮減未來財政赤字預期，進而

帶動10年期美債殖利率下滑，對長天期美債形成支撐，若共和黨持續全面掌控國會，市場可能預期減稅及財政擴張政策延續，
進一步推升財政赤字預估，帶動公債殖利率走高，對長天期美債形成壓力，不過市場已部分反映相關預期，實際衝擊幅度有限。
美國期中選舉前後對於美股的影響

AI建設熱潮吞噬自由現金流
• 資本支出創新高：雲端服務巨頭(包括亞馬遜、Alphabet、微軟、Meta、甲骨文以及SpaceX等)正在全力擴充AI算力與資料
中心，2026年大型雲端服務巨頭的資本支出合計預估將接近 9,600億美元，而2025至2031年的累計AI資本支出更可能逼近
9兆美元。
• 自由現金流出現赤字： 高額的投資加上股東回報(股息與庫藏股買回)，使許多巨頭的自由現金流(FCF)轉為負值(如Alphabet、
亞馬遜、微軟、Meta及甲骨文等)，預計到2027年，部分企業將更加依賴外部融資，而非單靠內部現金生成。
• 債券發行量大增： 為了填補資金缺口 ，科技業債券發行規模急遽上升 ，2026年迄今，各幣別的投資級科技債券發行量已突
破4,000億美元，創下歷史新高。

獲利韌性與金庫深度：強勁EBITDA成為信用安全網
• 上兆美元的流動性護城河： 儘管債務增加，但科技十二巨頭合計持有近 1兆美元的等值現金，整體科技板塊流動性充裕 ，短
期內無流動性危機。
• EBITDA強勁成長消化債務：市場預期覆蓋的12家最大科技企業在2026與2027年的EBITDA合計將成長約35%，強勁的獲利
能力顯著降低槓桿比率的上升壓力，確保信評機構維護穩定的信用評級。
• 資本配置策略分化：(1)兼顧股東回報：蘋果、微軟、Meta與Alphabet在投入AI同時，仍維持大規模的庫藏股買回與股息發
放。(2)全數投入資本支出：亞馬遜將絕大部分現金導向資本支出(2026年資本支出預計達2,200億美元居首)，且不發放股息。
• 小結與投資建議：科技巨頭為維持AI技術與基礎設施領先優勢，短期內，負自由現金流將成為常態。然而，這並非財務危機

訊號，而是企業利用超強獲利能力 (高EBITDA)與龐大的現金儲備進行戰略性擴張 ，科技板塊的信用風險目前處於可控且健
康的狀態，投資建議聚焦AI變現能力與EBITDA成長，優先佈局具有強大獲利護城河且能將算力轉化為實質營收的龍頭企業 。

越南股市正式納入富時羅素新興市場指數
• 預估引導60億美元資金流入越南： 富時羅素估計，此次納入最高可引導 60億美元的資金重新流入越南 。受此提振，資產管
理公司Vanguard也計畫在未來幾年內，將對越南的投資額提升至約25億美元。整個指數過渡將分為四個階段推行至2027年：
今年9月率先納入10%，明年3月追加20%，最後於明年6月與9月分別完成剩餘各35%的分配。市場分析指出，隨著明年3月
配額加大，市場關注度與對本地投資人的影響將更為顯著。
• 越南股市短期仍面臨逆風： 儘管官方早前誓言今年 GDP成長目標達10%，但中東衝突以來能源通膨壓力居高不下恐拖累經
濟成長。此外，越南與美國的貿易協商仍在進行中，由於今年以來越南成為美國最大貿易逆差國，加上美方企圖阻止越南成

為中國商品流入的中轉國，美對越的301條款調查為越南出口額帶來更多不確定性。
• 觀點：儘管短線面臨不確定性 ，但越南股市同時具備多項利多題材 ，包含企業獲利成長、公共投資加速、FDI回流及資本市
場改革等。中長期結構仍偏正向，可以衛星配置的角度布局，或是以定期定額的方式參與長期成長潛力。
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.09。

資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。

非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本

## Document 058: 2026年9月财政数据快评：收入向下，力度向上

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年9月财政数据快评：收入向下，力度向上.pdf
Report source: 国信证券
Date: 2026-10-25
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

事项：
前三季度，全国一般公共预算收入约 17.2万亿元，同比增长 5.1%。其中，全国税收收入约 14.1万亿元，
同比增长 6.1%。全国一般公共预算支出约 21.0万亿元，同比增长 1.0%。
评论：
9 月财政收入增速下滑，但政策力度指数继续回升。
第一本账收入转负，支出基本持平。9月收入同比下降 0.1%，较前值（4.7%）回落转负；税收收入中增值
税、个人所得税为主要拉动，消费税仍为最大单项拖累，企业所得税、证券交易印花税转负。支出当月同
比 0.03%，基本持平。后续随着基数抬高，收入将延续回落，支出端则预计逐渐向着预算增速追赶。
第二本账收入降幅再度扩大，支出降幅收窄。9 月第二本账收入同比-15.9%，土地出让收入同比-23.2%，
降幅均再度扩大，后续随着地产新政落地可能难有较大回升；支出同比下降 12.3%，降幅明显收窄，主要
是土地以外的其他支出降幅明显收窄，反映债务资金拨付有所加快。
总体来看，广义财政收支增速开始相向而行，力度指数连续回升。9月广义收入当月同比转负、广义支出

降幅明显收窄，二者增速差显著收窄，广义财政缺口同比收缩幅度也有所放缓。四季度只要增速达到 4.2%
以上，全年经济增速就能完成 4.5%的目标，5500 亿债务限额下达后，再出台增量政策的必要性并不算太
强。
风险提示：海外市场动荡，存在不确定性。
 一般公共预算：收入转负、支出持平
2026年10月24日

### Page 2

9 月财政收入小幅转负。1-9 月财政收入累计同比 5.1%，前值（1-8月）5.7%，有所回落。其中税收收入
累计 6.1%，前值 6.6%。单月来看，一般公共预算收入当月同比-0.1%，前值 4.7%，小幅转负。税收收入当
月同比 0.2%，前值 5.6%，连续第二个月回落；非税收入当月同比-1.0%，前值 0.3%，再度转负。税收显著
回落，主要是去年基数继续抬高叠加企业所得税等主体税种走弱；非税收入短暂转正后再度小幅负增长，
收入端整体进入趋势性回落通道，预计后续可能会进一步回落，逐渐靠近年初预算的收入目标（2.2%）。

主要税种增速分化，增值税回升、消费税仍为最大拖累。主要税种中增值税同比 7.3%，较前值（4.0%）回升，

拉动税收收入 3.1个百分点、为最大拉动项；个人所得税同比 10.5%，较前值（11.8%）小幅回落，拉动税
收 1.1个百分点，累计同比 14.1%，仍在高速增长区间；企业所得税同比-0.4%，较前值（11.6%）明显回
落转负；消费税同比-8.5%，降幅较前值（-14.0%）收窄，拖累税收 1.0个百分点、仍为最大的单项拖累。
房地产相关税种整体降幅由-1.9%明显扩大至-14.6%，其中契税（-16.9%）仍然疲弱，土地增值税（-22.2%）
再度转负。此外，证券交易印花税同比-11.5%（前值 17.9%），在 2025 年 9 月同比 342%的超高基数上转
负，拖累税收 0.3 个百分点，后续可能随成交量走弱持续降低；进口环节增值税和消费税同比 12.9%，拉
动税收 1.9个百分点，与增速较高的进口数据较为一致。

### Page 3

图5：各税种对税收收入的拉动排序 图6：主要税种同比增速
资料来源:Wind，国信证券经济研究所整理 资料来源:Wind，国信证券经济研究所整理
支出端与上月基本持平。一般公共支出当月同比 0.03%，较前值（-0.1%）小幅回升。节奏上，一般公共预
算支出当月完成 9.6%，与去年同期（9.7%）基本一致。累计来看，1-9月第一本账支出增速为 1.0%，累计
进度 70.0%，与去年同期（70.1%）大体相当；但离 4.4%的预算支出增速水平差距仍然较大，后续有待存
量资金加快拨付带动支出增速回升，全年增速预计回升到 2-3%之间。
结构上，民生支出转负、基建降幅收窄。民生类支出同比增速-0.9%，较前值（1.9%）回落转负。其中，
教育支出同比增长 3.5%，前值 1.3%；社保就业支出-4.8%，前值 2.6%；卫生健康支出同比-2.9%，较前值
（3.8%）回落；文化娱乐体育传媒支出增速 1.6%，由负转正（前值-9.6%）。基建类支出同比增速-1.3%，
前值-3.7%，拖累支出 0.3个百分点。其中交通运输支出同比-10.8%，前值-5.8%，降幅扩大；城乡社区事

务支出增速由前值-3.8%转正至 7.9%；农林水事务支出同比-0.2%，较前值（1.1%）小幅回落；环保支出-13.7%，
降幅继续收窄（前值-15.8%）。此外，债务付息支出同比-1.2%，较前值（7.0%）转负；科学技术支出同
比 6.9%，较前值（9.0%）回落，仍维持较快增长。

 政府性基金预算：收入降幅再度扩大、支出降幅收窄
9 月政府性基金收入同比-15.9%（前值-3.8%），降幅再度扩大。土地出让收入（-23.2%）拖累重新加重，
同样较前值（-12.6%）明显扩大。政府性基金支出同比-12.3%，降幅较前值（-21.4%）明显收窄，主要是
除土地出让收入相关支出外的其他支出降幅由-18.0%收窄至-8.4%，反映专项债、特别国债资金拨付有所
加快。
1-9月，第二本账收入累计增速为-18.6%，支出增速为-16.2%。其中土地出让收入 16087 亿，同比-27.9%，
降幅较 1-8月（-28.6%）收窄，但仍处深度负增长区间，按当前降幅外推，全年仍不及 3 万亿。后续来看，

随着专项债、超长期特别国债资金拨付加快与新型政策性金融工具持续投放，第二本账支出端有望延续修
复。

 总结：财政力度指数继续回升
财政力度指数连续第二个月回升。一方面是收入转负的同时支出降幅收窄。9月广义支出增速-3.7%，较前
值-6.7%明显收窄；广义收入当月同比增速-3.5%，前值 2.9%，由正转负。1-9月，广义财政支出累计增速
为-3.5%，完成度 65.2%，低于去年同期（67.1%）；广义财政收入累计增速为 1.4%，完成度 70.8%。另一
方面是赤字端同比收缩幅度放缓。1-9 月累计的广义财政缺口为 7.6万亿，较 2025年同期（8.8万亿）减
少约 1.3 万亿，同比收缩 14.4%，收缩幅度较 1-8 月（17.7%）有所收窄。此外，9 月新增财政存款-4300
亿，季末支出加快带动财政存款季节性回落，月末财政存款余额 7.9万亿；当月社融口径政府债券净融资
1.54万亿，较前值（1.01 万亿）明显回升。9月财政力度指数回升，连续第二个月回升。

后续来看，财政收支的 K 型分化将继续收敛。9月广义财政收入与支出增速已开始相向而行，政府债净融
资明显回升，8000 亿新型政策性金融工具持续投放，四季度专项债和超长期特别国债剩余额度发行使用仍
在途中。预计广义财政支出增速有望继续回升，关注政府债资金使用节奏与实物工作量形成情况。此外，
四季度只要增速达到 4.2%以上，全年经济增速就能完成 4.5%的目标，出台增量政策的必要性并不算太强，
现在的问题并非缺钱而是落地端。

### Page 1

Improving 3Q GPM helped by better mix — Dynapack's preliminary 3Q26 results 
showed margin recovery yet weak PC sales remained the key drag on the earnings. 
3Q26 EPS of NT$2.33 (+72% q/q and +49% y/y) missed our/BBG est by 45%/27%, 
although we believe the earnings shortfall was well anticipated given the 3Q sales 
miss owing to the delayed shipment of one of the BBU products and weaker PC 
demand. GPM improved q/q on a richer product mix, with higher-margin BBU 
making up c.50% of 3Q sales on our estimate, while OPM recovered to 10.7%, 
+4.2ppt q/q and +1.3ppt y/y. Nevertheless, the sales shortfall constrained op 
leverage, leaving margin improvement insufficient to offset weaker sales. 
PSU vendor shortages weigh on Oct; 12kW ramp to drive Nov sales rebound — 
Our industry checks suggest that Dynapack's major PSU vendor has encountered 
component supply shortages (related to power management IC) and therefore 
delayed BBU pull-ins, weighing on shipments of Dynapack's existing 3kW and 5kW 
BBU products in Oct. We therefore expect Oct-26 BBU sales to decline slightly m/m. 
Meanwhile, PC sales should recover sequentially and more than to offset the BBU 
softness, supporting a slight m/m increase in group sales. Looking beyond Oct-26, 
we expect accelerating shipments of the new 12kW BBU series from Nov-26 to drive 
a strong rebound in BBU sales.

Implications — We reiterate our bullish stance on the BBU sector despite near-term
volatility from external supply-chain constraints, which is likely to put pressure on
near-term sentiment. We view the component-driven delays in product pull-ins
primarily as a shipment-timing issue, with underlying demand remaining strong into
2027E. We also see ample opportunity for Dynapack to gain market share from

Panasonic, its major Japanese peer, particularly as power architectures migrate
toward HVDC. This share-gain potential remains intact, reinforcing our confidence
in Dynapack's medium term BBU growth outlook.

### Page 4

Dynapack International (3211.TWO)
19 October 2026 Citi Research
4
Dynapack International
Valuation
We assign a 32x target P/E to our 2027E EPS of NT$25 to derive a target price of NT$800. We value Dynapack based on 2027E
target P/E as we believe 2027E better reflects its earnings power after BBU capacity expansion. Our target P/E of 32x is based
on our forecast EPS CAGR of 66% in 2025-2027E and a PEG of 0.5x, which we benchmark against its major PSU partner, as we

expect Dynapack to grow alongside its partner.
Risks
Our quantitative model assigns a High Risk rating to Dynapack. However, we believe such a rating is not warranted for
Dynapack due to: 1) the company is generating solid revenue and earnings growth over next 3 years; the underlying industry,
BBU for AIDC, also enjoys secular growth; 2) the business model that involves with product customization and strict
safety/reliability requirements would imply relatively benign competition and therefore stable pricing and margins; and 3) the

company has a strong balance sheet and has been in net cash position since 2019.
Key downside risks to our price target/rating are: 1) Slower-than-expected BBU adoption in AIDC, 2) Delay in PSU customer
qualifications, 3) Competition from larger battery or power solution vendors, 4) Safety, certification and reliability issues.
Panasonic Holdings
(6752.T; ¥3957.0; 1; 16 Oct 26; 15:30)
Valuation
To derive our target price, we use a global sum-of-the-parts (SoTP) approach. In the case of Panasonic Holdings, we reference

52 other companies in the Citi coverage universe to set a fair value PER for each business division, which we sum to derive our
overall target price.
Panasonic fair-value PERs for individual business segments range from 8x to 31x, and have a weighted average of 26.2x
(excluding subsidies in the battery business), taking minority interest and equity in earnings into account. We estimate
business-derived equity value of ¥5,000/share. We calculate the present value of the amount we expect Panasonic to receive

in battery subsidies over five years at ¥400/share. Combining these two values, we derive a target price of ¥5,400.
Risks
Risks to our target price include 1) unsuccessful management policies or strategies resulting in weaker-than-expected
business investment returns or sales growth, or conversely producing greater-than-expected results at a relatively early stage,
2) stagnation in the global economy or an upturn in it, and rises or declines in demand in the electrical industry, and 3) forex

trends. If these factors manifest themselves differently than we have anticipated, the share price may vary from our target
price.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

주가 추이

3Q 큰 폭의 판매 감소, 매출/영업이익 시장 기대치 하회 예상 
3Q26 동사 매출액과 영 업 이 익 은 각각 44.6조원(-4.5% 이하 Y oY)과 2.4조원
(-5.4%) 기록하며 시장 기대치 하회 전망. 3Q 매출액은 글로벌 판매 부진 지속
과 환율 하락, 그리고 친환경차 판매 성장 둔화 등의 영향에 기인. 특히, 3Q 판
매량은 918.7만대(-11.5% 이하 Y oY)로 전년 동기 대비 큰 폭 감소하며 매출
액에 부정적 영향. 이는 7월과 8월 발생한 파업 영향으로 약 5.5만대 규모의 생
산 물량 차질이 발 생 한 부분에 기인. 파업은 기존 물량/믹스 개선에 기 여 했 던 
글로벌 HEV 판매도 둔화(+3.4%)시키면서 매출에 부정적 영향을 심화. 
3Q 수 익 성 은 4~5월 상승했던 원자재 투입에 따른 원가 상승 영향과 친환경차 
판매 감소에 따른 부정적 믹스 영향 불 가 피. 이에 시장 기대치인 3.0조원을 크
게 하회할 전망. 다만, 기말 환율이 1,356원/$ 수준으로 낮아지면서 판매보증비 
외화환산손익 영향은 다소 개선(0.4%p) 예상되며, 관세 영향 또한 전년 동기 
대비 약 1조원(2.1%p) 감소하면서 수익성 악화 영향을 일부 만 회 할 전망.  
’27년 지연된 신차효과 가시화로 반등 여력 충분 
4Q부터는 3Q 파업 영향 등으로 지연된 신차 확대 본격화되고, 현지 수요 높은 
차종 중심의 물량 확대를 통해 매출 회복 및 이익 성장 가시화가 기대. 아 반 떼
와 투싼 FMC 확대로 국내 및 해외 판매 둔화가 점진적 만회되고, 지난 9월부
터 터키에서 아이오닉3 생산이 본격화되면서 유럽에서의 시장 부진 회복이 기
대. 또한, GV80과 G80 HEV 모델 출시로 내년부터 국내/북미 중심 판매 확대
되며 올해 부진했던 물량/믹스 개선에 기여할 것으로 기대. ’27년 HMGMA에
서 HEV 신차 생산 투입 예정되어 있어 상 대 적 으 로 높은 수요가 지속되는 북미
에서의 HEV 현지 생산 확대로 가동률 증 가 와 관세 부담 완화도 수익성 개선에 
기여할 것으로 기대. 
부정적 환율 고려 필요하나 ’27년 강한 증익 기대  
’27년 동사 영 업 이 익 추 정 치 는 12.4조원(+22.3%)으로 ’26년 감 익 에 서 ’27년 
증익 전환 예상. 최근 환율 추세 고려 시 내년 상 반 기 까 지 부정적인 환율 영향 
확대 가 능 성 존재하나, 기존 노후 차 종 에 서 현지 수요 대응 가능한 신차 중심 
생산/판매 확대로 물량/믹스 개선되며 부 정 적 환율 영향 상쇄 기대.  
12M EPS 추정치 하향(-11.8%)과 상반기 적 용 한 코 스 피 프리미엄 제거(-
10%)에 따라 목 표 주 가 를 670,000원으로 하향. 그러나, 연 내 RMAC 운영 
성 과 와 RA 설립, 12 월 모셔널 자 율 주 행 상용화와 웨 이 모向 로 보 택 시 
양산/공급 등 미래 사 업 에 서 의 가 시 성 도 여전히 기대 가능한 부분.

[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

## Document 197: AIを活用する消費者に企業はどう向き合うか～生成AI利用者の３割半ばが家族・友人より先にAIへ相談

Source PDF: all report pdf/AIを活用する消費者に企業はどう向き合うか～生成AI利用者の３割半ばが家族・友人より先にAIへ相談.pdf
Report source: NLI
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1――はじめに ～ AIとの相談で生まれた消費者の期待を、企業は店頭でどう受け止めるか

1｜はじめに ～ AIが消費者の意思決定に与える影響とリスクを考える 
内閣府消費者委員会の「人工知能（AI）技術の利用と消費者問題に関する専門調査会」では、AIが
消費者の意思決定に与える影響とリスクが検討されている。事務局の検討状況案によれば、個人に合
わせたAIの働きかけによって、消費者自身が影響に気づかないまま判断し、問題があっても相談や救
済につながりにくい可能性が、課題とされている。

たとえば、商品選びでも、AIとの相談によりニーズが整理される一方、AIから提供される情報の偏
りや見落としに気づかず、購入後に予想外の費用や手間を知る場合が考えられる。 
本稿では、専門調査会のこうした問題意識を踏まえ、消費者の購入前のAIとの相談、店舗やサイト
での説明、使用後の経験のつながりに注目し、商品の使い方や必要な手間について、企業の説明や対
応で何を補えるか、使用後の経験をどのような改善に活かせるかを考えていく。

2｜生成 AI利用者の35.0％が、家族・友人より先にAIへ相談する 
総務省「令和8年版情報通信白書」によれば、日本の生成AI利用経験は、20歳以上で2024年度調
査の 26.7％から2025年度調査の52.2％へ上昇した。1また、消費者庁「令和 8年版消費者白書」2で
は、商品・サービス購入を支援するAIを、検索や商品の比較に使う実態も示されている。AIとの事
前相談を経て店舗やサイトを訪れる消費者は今後、一層増えていくことが予想される。 
また、内閣府による生成 AI利用者の利用実態調査の結果3によれば、生成 AI利用者のうち 35.0％

1 総務省. (2026). 令和 8年版情報通信白書（第Ⅰ部第1章第 1節、図表Ⅰ-1-1-1）. 白書の数値は生成AI全般の利用経験を示している。 
2 消費者庁. (2026). 令和 8年版消費者白書（第Ⅰ部第 2章第 2節、図表Ⅰ-2-2-16）. 
3 内閣府消費者委員会事務局. (2026). 生成 AI利用者の利用実態に関するアンケート結果。2026年 2月 16～18日実施／インターネット調
査／日本在住10歳以上の生成AI利用者1442人が対象。男女×7年代に各103人を割り付け（人口構成に比例した標本ではない）

3｜AIとの事前相談は、自分が欲しい機種の整理に繋がるのか 
「令和8年版情報通信白書」でも、 「日常の生活に伴う購入を検討・判断するとき」に何かしらの形
でAIを利用する人は、全体の37.4％と４割近くにものぼる。4 
消費者による商品購入時のAIへの相談場面としては、商品を選ぶときに、AIに予算や使う場所を
伝えて、おすすめを聞いたり、候補を比べたりする、といった使い方が考えられる。 
たとえば、この具体的なケースとして、家電の「掃除機」の買い替えをイメージしてみたい。 
ある消費者が自宅の部屋の広さなど購入要件としてAIに伝えるうちに、 「吸引力も気になるが、ゴ
ミ捨てやフィルター清掃などの手入れを減らしたい」と新たな希望がはっきりしてくる場合がある。 
その過程で、そのような購入要件や候補機種を整理できれば、その後で店舗を訪問したときに店員
に対して相談しやすくなるだろう。 
その一方で、 その消費者が候補とした掃除機について、 「ゴミを捨てる時など、 使用時の手入れが楽」
というAIからの事前説明を受けて、実際には手入れが必要であるにもかかわらず、 「ほとんど手入れ
が要らない」と受け止めていたらどうだろうか。 
購入後に、使って初めて必要な作業の手間を知り、戸惑うことがあるかもしれない。無論、購入前
に、その作業を正確に知っていれば、別の機種を選んだ可能性もありうる。

2――消費者が商品を選ぶ理由は、相談の中でも形づくられる

4 総務省. (2026). 令和 8年版情報通信白書（第Ⅰ部第 1章第 1節、図表Ⅰ-1-1-12） 「日常の生活に伴う購入を検討・判断するとき」の
AI利用の数値。なおAIのみは2.9％、主にAIは 10.1％、主に他の方法でAIを補助的に利用は24.4％（合計37.4％）となる。 
資料：　内閣府消費者委員会事務局（2026）計は表示した2区分の合計。

### Page 3

しかし、問題は、AIとの対話で自分が重視する点が整理され、特定の商品やサービスへの購入意向
が高まったとしても、その商品が本当に自分の暮らしに適しているとは限らないことである。 
また、消費者に対するAIの影響について、冒頭で紹介した専門調査会の事務局資料でも、たとえば
AIの質問や話し方が、消費者の好みや希望の形成に影響する可能性として指摘されている。7 
また、消費者行動のある関連研究によれば、消費者の商品について重視する点が、購入・使用前は
「機能の豊富さ」 、使用後には「使いやすさ」という様に変化する傾向が見られると言う。8

これを掃除機の例で言えば、AIと「性能面」を中心に相談して商品を選んだ後、実際の使用時には、
事前相談で重視していなかった持ち運びや手入れが気になる、といったケースが考えられる。

3――利用実態の数字のみならず、購入まで・利用後までの経過を見る

1｜「生成 AIの回答をそのまま受け入れてしまうことが多い」が34.3％ 
それでは、消費者の視点からは、AIの回答はどのように受け止められているのだろうか。 
前掲の専門調査会で紹介された研究によれば、AIからの説明を、利用者が重視する点に合わせて提
供すると、ある予測課題で説明への満足度などが高まったことが示されている。9 
また、別の関連する研究では、AIが回答に理由を添えると、正しい回答だけでなく、誤った回答も

品理解の研究ではな点に注意。

### Page 4

また先掲の内閣府の調査によれば、生成AI利用者のうち、AIの回答をそのまま受け入れてしまう
ことが多いという回答が34.3％と全体の約１/３、そして、AIの回答に納得できなくても自分の意見
を変えることがあるという回答が20.5％であった。 
この結果は、商品やサービスの購入に限らず、日常生活全般での生成AI利用について尋ねたもので
あり、回答を受け入れたり意見を変えたりした理由、 その結果が本人に役立ったかまでは分からない。 
ただし、AIの回答をそのまま受け入れたり、納得できなくても自分の意見を変えたりする利用者が
一定数いることは伺える結果である。

図
表 
 2 
 生成
 AI
利用者の回答受容・意見変更に関する実感

2｜相談 AIの対話の自然さや人間らしさも高まりつつある  
さらに、昨今の生成AIを用いた相談サービスでは、対話の自然さや人間らしさも高まりつつある。 
関連する研究では、AIの人間らしい反応が利用者の信頼を高め得ることや、AIによる説明や説得
が、内容の正誤とは必ずしも一致しない形で利用者の信頼に影響する可能性も指摘されている。11 
これは、AIによる説明が分かりやすく、もっともらしく感じられていれば、その内容が誤っていて
も、 消費者が誤りに気づかないまま、 誤った理解に基づき選択する可能性があることを示唆している。 
今後、相談AIの対人性が高まるほど、その利用促進と併せて、利用者がAIに寄せる信頼が、AIの
実際の能力や回答の確かさに適切に対応しているかも確認する必要があると言えるだろう。

10 Kim, S. S. Y ., Vaughan, J. W., Liao, Q. V ., Lombrozo, T., & Russakovsky, O. (2025). Fostering appropriate reliance on large language 
models: The role of explanations, sources, and inconsistencies. In Proceedings of the 2025 CHI Conference on Human Factors in 
Computing Systems. Association for Computing Machinery . 
11 Tan, L., Li, Z., Huang, Y., Liu, J., Ni, M., Wang, S., Che, J., & Chen, K. (2026). DHCBT: A digital human psychological counseling 
system with a CBT multi-agent framework. Scientific Reports. Advance online publication. 
人間らしい相談インターフェースのシステムが、テキスト型システムより、利用者に「相手がそこにいる」という感覚を強く与え、継続利
用したいという評価を高めた。

### Page 5

ここまで見てきたように、消費者の期待と、製品・サービスの実際の仕様や使い方に違いがあると、
必要な情報を十分に知らないまま購入し、想定した用途に使えなかったり、予想外の費用や負担が生
じたりする可能性がある。そして、この問題は、消費者が十分な情報を得て商品を選べたかという、
消費者支援上の課題にもつながっていく。 
さらに、企業にとっても、場合によっては満足度やNPS（Net Promoter Score）などの顧客評価が
下がるリスクになり得る。

この点を、企業の店頭やサイト運営者の視点から見ると、AIとの事前相談を経た顧客のニーズや期
待を、サイトでの説明や店頭での接客において、どのように受け止めるかという問いにもつながる。 
下表（表1）は、AIとの相談を経た消費者に生じ得る情報の偏りや見落としについて、関連研究を
踏まえ、確認の着眼点として整理したものである。

表 
 1 
 AI
との事前相談を経た商品選びで、確かめたい偏り・見落とし

このように、消費者がAIとの事前相談を経たとしても、情報の偏りや見落としが残る場合がある。 
しかも、企業はその相談内容を十分に把握できるとは限らない。仮に、「企業が提供する AI」を通
じて生まれた期待であれば、設計や権限によっては、相談内容や履歴を参照し、説明を点検する手掛
かりを得られるだろう。一方、顧客が個人で使うChatGPTなどの汎用的なAIサービスでは、その相談
内容を企業が十分に確認できるとは限らない。 
また、AIとの事前相談内容を「個人的なもの」と感じて、事業者への開示に抵抗感を示す消費者も
いると思われる。AIとの事前相談の内容を詳しく聞くことにこだわらず、本人がストレスを感じるこ
となく話せる範囲の情報をもとに、必要な対応を検討していく視点も必要であろう。

着眼点 
  偏り・見落としの可能性
おすすめの前提
 AIに伝えた用途や予算、使う環境が十分でなく、
おすすめが本人の実情に合っていない。
比較する候補
 AIが薦めた商品だけを検討し、自分に合うほかの選択肢が
比較から抜けている。
比較する基準
 価格や性能を中心に比べ、重さ、収納、手入れなど、
 暮らしに重要な点を見落としている。
情報の正確さ
 説明に納得していても、商品の仕様や注意点に誤りや
 抜けがないかを確かめていない。
日常での使い方
 機能や仕様の違いは分かっていても、使う際の作業や手間、
 継続してかかる費用まで想像できていない。
　出典：Häubl & Trifts（2000）、Häubl & Murray（2003）、Thompson et al.（2005）、Kim et al.（2025）を踏まえ
 　　　　 ニッセイ基礎研究所で整理。

### Page 6

その前提に立ったとき、企業はどのような打ち手が考えられるのだろうか。 
まず、消費者自身に、AIとの事前相談を通して重視した点を振り返り、自分の暮らしに大切な点が
比較に含まれていたかを、自ら確かめる、気づける機会を提供していく方法が考えられる。 
例えば、掃除機を例とすれば、機種を価格や吸引力で選んだと話す消費者に対して、本体の重さ、
収納時に必要なスペース、ゴミ捨てやフィルター清掃などについても比較したかを、必要に応じて具
体的に尋ねるような方法で想定される。 
さらに、期待と実際に違いがありそうな点を、図や動画、具体的な使用場面で説明することも、消
費者自身が違いに気づくきっかけになり得る。掃除機なら、ゴミ捨てやフィルター清掃を実演して見
せることで、 「想像より手間がかかる」と分かる場合があるだろう。 
これらはいずれも、従来の顧客対応の延長上で対処できるアプローチでもあるとも言える。

もう１つは、企業でよく行われている消費者（顧客）に対する満足度やNPS（Net Promoter Score）
といった顧客調査の活用する方法も考えられる。 
店舗やサイトで説明を受ける直前（AIとの事前相談をしていれば、その後）に、その商品をどのよ
うに使えると思っていたか（事前期待） 、購入時の店頭やサイトの説明で分かったこと（理解） 、使用
後に感じた期待との違い（事前期待の実態の差についての気づき）を聞く。そして、事前のAI相談に
ついては、その有無のみを併せて聞く。 
これにより、事前のAI相談が期待にどう影響し、期待をどう形成したかまでは分からないものの、
企業にとって商品の改善が必要なのか、店頭やサイトでの説明や相談を補う必要があるのか、を考え
やすくなる。消費者支援の施策面でも、消費者にとってどのような場面で情報が不足しがちなのか、
既存の説明や相談の過程のどこを見直せるかを知る材料にはなる。

こうしたアプローチは、購入時・使用時への対応を中心としてきた従来の顧客へのアプローチに対
して、自社との接触前に行われる「消費者とAIとの事前相談」までをスコープに組み入れ、相談、購
入時の説明、使用後の経験を一体として捉える対応とも言えるだろう。

本稿では、消費者によるAIへの事前相談を出発点に、店舗やサイトでの説明、使用後の経験を一体
として考え、今後の消費者への支援に活かすアプローチに着目してきた。 
次稿では生命保険の調査と聞き取りを取り上げ、既存の説明や意向確認との関係から、この課題を
より具体化して考えていく。

本資料記載のデータは各種の情報源から入手・加工したものであり、その正確性と完全性を保証するものではありません。 
また、本資料は情報提供が目的であり、記載の意見や予測は、いかなる契約の締結や解約を勧誘するものではありません。

## Document 257: ECB caught in the French drama

Source PDF: all report pdf/ECB caught in the French drama.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The European Central Bank meeting on 29 October is not
expected to result in an interest rate hike. Instead, the French
crisis is increasingly eroding the need for additional rate hikes
Carsten Brzeski
With the surge in bond yields on the back of the crisis in France, we expect the
ECB to remain on hold at next week's meeting
Back in September, the ECB surprised markets with a more hawkish
message, at least between the lines. With headline inﬂation higher

for longer and core inﬂation expected to increase in 2027, the stage
seemed set for at least one more rate hike at the December
meeting. Some even speculated about a back-to-back increase in
October.
One month later, and after a severe ﬁscal and political crisis in
France, the ECB is unlikely to repeat the hawkish tone at next week’s
meeting. At face value, macro developments since the last meeting
would still justify another rate hike. Headline inﬂation increased

again in September and most leading indicators point to continued
resilience in the eurozone economy. However, the surge in bond

### Page 2

yields on the back of the French crisis is already imposing a new
restrictive monetary policy stance. In fact, the surge in German
bond yields since the September meeting has already had roughly
the same impact on growth and inﬂation as an additional 25bp
rate hike. For France, it's closer to a 50bp hike. Or in other words, the
bond market has taken over the ECB’s job.
All of this means that the ECB is very likely to stay on hold next
week and will delay any next rate hike decision to December. This is

when new staﬀ projections will be available and when there will
also be more insights into whether higher energy prices will
eventually bring knock-on eﬀects on the rest of the economy or
not. In fact, there are three main parameters that, in our view, will
determine the ECB’s path going forward: i) the length and intensity
of the war in the Middle East. The longer oil prices remain elevated,
the higher the probability of more rate hikes; ii) the knock-on eﬀects

of higher energy prices. So far, there has hardly been any pass-
through of higher energy prices in the rest of the economy. If this
remains the case, the need for further rate hikes drops. If it doesn’t,
the likelihood of new rate hikes goes up. iii) France. Any solution to
the current French ﬁscal woes could bring some relief to bond
markets and allow the ECB to continue hiking rates. Without any
solution, bond markets have taken over the ECB’s role, making rate

hikes less likely (and less needed).
Because it's France
Even if France does not play a role in the ECB’s policy statement
next week, it will be an important element at the press conference.
Political and ﬁscal concerns in France, and the resulting widening in
French spreads, have raised questions about whether the ECB could
intervene through the Transmission Protection Instrument (TPI),
which allows it to purchase securities issued by member states

whose ﬁnancing conditions have deteriorated for reasons not
justiﬁed by country-speciﬁc fundamentals. However, before
activating it, the Governing Council considers a cumulative set of
criteria to determine whether the countries concerned are pursuing
sound and sustainable ﬁscal and macroeconomic policies. In
practice, this includes compliance with the EU ﬁscal framework,

which would, at a minimum, require the proposed French budget to
be fully adopted.
The ECB has nevertheless left itself some discretion, stating that the
intervention criteria “will be dynamically adjusted to the unfolding
risks and conditions to be addressed”. This brings back memories of
the famous “because it’s France” by former European Commission
President Jean-Claude Juncker. Generally speaking, the ECB will be
in a diﬃcult position: intervening too quickly in support of France

may be viewed unfavourably by other member states and
interpreted as monetary ﬁnancing of the debt of a country
unwilling to follow the rules. That argues strongly against early
intervention. Also, don’t forget that the leadership changes at the
ECB over the next months (Schnabel, Lane, Lagarde) could also
aﬀect how the ECB views TPI.
Given the challenges of justifying the activation of TPI at present, we
expect the ECB to adopt a three-pronged approach to address the

French crisis.
1. The ﬁrst line of defence is a more dovish approach to policy
rates. As bond markets are currently taking over the ECB’s job
of inserting a more restrictive monetary stance on the
economy, there is less need for the ECB to hike rates
aggressively, or to hike rates at all. In fact, the ECB has already
started to prepare for this option, as ECB president Christine
Lagarde recently noted that “while growth has been resilient,
since our last meeting long-term interest rates have risen

notably, which will slow growth and reduce pass-through by
more than projected in our September exercise".
2. The second line of defence would be a covert return to bond
purchases. The ECB could instead pause quantitative tightening
temporarily and reinvest maturing bonds in its portfolio
'ﬂexibly', sending a positive signal to bond markets. This had
already happened during the Covid period.
3. Last but not least, should contagion in eurozone bond markets

intensify, we expect the ECB to eventually trigger the TPI. As
long as France remains non-compliant with the European ﬁscal

## Document 269: Eurozone_ Improving business sentiment, driven by tech, is spreading to other sectors (2026-10-14)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Eurozone_ Improving business sentiment, driven by tech, is spreading to other sectors (2026-10-14).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-14
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

14 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
EUROZONE: IMPROVING BUSINESS SENTIMENT, DRIVEN BY TECH, IS SPREADING TO OTHER SECTORS
Stéphane Colliac & Tarik Rharrab
*LATEST OBSERVATION: 5 OCTOBER 2026  **ESTIMATE
The September PMI surveys confirm that growth in the Eurozone is getting stronger, boosted in particular by new export orders, which are
still rising. Sectors that were already performing well – technology and capital goods (including for defence) – are continuing to grow, while

those that had been struggling until now – aeronautics, consumer goods and financial services – are seeing their production indices recover.
This strong performance is particularly noteworthy, given that it has not yet been undermined by higher inflation.
The heatmap shows the PMI index readings: a reading above 50 indicates expansion, while a reading below 50 signals contraction compared to the previous
month. For each indicator, sectors are ranked in descending order based on their September 2026 reading.

Shades of red represent values above 50, while shades of blue represent values below 50: the intensity of the colour increases the further away they are
from this threshold.
Production growth spreads to more sectors
The PMI surveys on business sentiment confirm that business activity in the Eurozone is expanding at a faster pace and spreading to more sectors.
Household-consumption-related sectors (goods and services), which were adversely affected in the spring by the outbreak of the conflict in the Middle

East, do not appear to be negatively impacted by the rising inflation observed since August. The aeronautics sector, where production has mainly been
limited by supply constraints, is also showing signs of improvement.
SOURCE: S&P GLOBAL, BNP PARIBAS

These trends, although still moderate, come alongside a drive to upgrade equipment, fuelled by investment in digital technology, electrification
(which is supporting the automotive sector) and defence. The momentum in technology goods and services further strengthened in September.
The production index for computer and electronics hit a five-year high, while the software services production index stayed close to its July peak.

Financial services are also enjoying a significant improvement, reflecting the healthy lending momentum despite interest rates starting to rise
(see our analysis).
Growth driven by exports
Exports drove growth in the first half of 2026, both across the Eurozone as a whole (see our analysis) and among its key members (notably
Germany). This momentum is also increasing, as indicated by the ‘new export orders’ component of the various PMI indices. As was the case with

production, there has been a particularly noteworthy increase for capital goods (technology and machinery) and related services, particularly digital
services. This trend is playing out more broadly, as, in September, new export orders rose across all sub-sectors, with the exception of intermediate
goods and consumer goods, where contraction recently eased. There has been a significant improvement in the transport sector, particularly in
the aeronautics industry, which has major markets in Asia. Recent data on French exports show that aeronautics exports to this region are rising

once again.
The convergence between rising production and increasing export-order backlogs shows that Eurozone growth is based on an increasingly solid
foundation. Inflationary pressures are not holding it back for the time being.

## Document 279: France - 2027 Budget_ Fiscal consolidation must now resume (2026-10-01)

Source PDF: all report pdf/France - 2027 Budget_ Fiscal consolidation must now resume (2026-10-01).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 1st October 2026

EcoFlash

2027 Budget: Fiscal consolidation must now resume

The 2027 budget, adopted by the French Council of Ministers today, calls for a reduction in France’s fiscal 
deficit to 5% of GDP in 2027 (down from 5.4% in 2026). This is a first step toward s the multi-year fiscal 
consolidation needed to stabilise the public debt-to-GDP ratio. This ratio is expected to rise further in 2027 
and until the deficit is reduced to 3% of GDP. Furthermore, given the increase in some expenditures (+0.6 pp 
of GDP for debt service and defence spending), the projected fiscal effort amounts to 1 pp. Nearly one-third 
of this effort would come from higher compulsory levies, and two -thirds from curbing government and 
social spending.

2027 Draft finance law: A step in the right direction. The government is forecasting an improvement in the fiscal 
deficit from 5.4% of GDP in 2026 to 5% in 2027. This projection is based on a credible growth assumption of 1% 
(see our analysis of French growth drivers). To achieve this target, compulsory levies (CL) are expected to rise to 
44.2% of GDP, an increase of 0.3 pp (the same increase as in 2026, compared with +0.9 pp in 2025). The public 
spending-to-GDP ratio would decline by 0.2 pp to 56.9%. The effort required to reduce the deficit to 5% is greater 
than it appears. Indeed, some expenditures will increase (interest payments by 0.4 pp and defence spending by 
0.2 pp). Consequently, the fiscal effort for next year amounts not to 0.4 pp, but to 1 pp of GDP. One-third of this will 
be achieved through increased CL, and two-thirds through spending cuts. Social spending will contribute, notably 
through the cancellation of EUR 3 billion in social contribution reductions for low-wage earners, as well as efforts 
totaling EUR 6 billion each in the areas of pensions and healthcare. Social spending shows the largest deviation 
from pre-COVID levels, with an increase representing 1.2 pp of GDP, nearly half of the gap between the 2026 
budget balance and a 3% deficit. Government spending, excluding interest payments and defence, is expected to 
be frozen in nominal terms.

A compromise seems likely despite a tight schedule. The French National Assembly will start reviewing the 
budget on Tuesday 13 October. The government will need to ensure that a majority vote against the draft budget 
does not form. The current circumstances, including increasing sovereign bond yields and the upcoming 
presidential election, call for a consensus based on fiscal consolidation of the size proposed by the government. If 
Parliament does not pass a budget by the end of the year, the government will have to choose between 
implementing the government’s budget proposal by executive order ( only possible if there was no parliamentary 
vote) or through a special law (as in 2025 and 2026). A special law would enable tax collection , while the 
government and social security agencies could continue to issue debt. In this case, the 2026 budget would apply 
in 2027 (without indexation) until Parliament passes a budget in the first few weeks of the year. This must occur 
before the end of February (when Parliament’s session ends, earlier than usual due to the presidential election).

The one-year postponement of the 5% budget deficit target has a measurable impact. The gap between the 
budget adopted for 2026 (deficit of 5% of GDP) and the actual budget outcome (deficit of 5.4%) is due to 
unforeseeable circumstances (the conflict in Iran  and extreme drought). However, postponing the deficit target  
previously set for 2026 to 2027 comes at a cost: 1) the public debt-to-GDP ratio is expected to reach 121% in 2027 
according to our forecasts (1 pp above our scenario with a 5% target in 2026 and a deficit reduced to 4.5% in 2027), 
and 2) the (upcoming) postponement to 2032 of the 3% of GDP deficit target (a commitment that most presidential 
candidates plan to make) rather than in 2029 (the government had committed to this with the European Commission 
in 2024). In 2032, the public debt-to-GDP ratio would stabilise at 124% of GDP (instead of 121.5% if the deficit had 
been reduced to 5% of GDP as early as 2026 and to 3% of GDP by the end of the decade).

Another delay in fiscal consolidation would further erode France’s credibility. This new delay could stem 
either from a decision by the government formed in May 2027 to immediately implement part of its agenda, or from 
the enactment of a special law that would remain in effect until (just after) the likely legislative elections in June  
2027  or  for  the  entire  year.  If  the  government  emerging  from  the  elections  were  to  resume  fiscal  
consolidation only in 2028 (with a deficit of 5% of GDP that it projects to reduce to 3% by 2032), public debt would 
reach 126% of GDP by that date (a one-year delay in fiscal consolidation would result in an additional 2 pp of 
debt). This further delay could potentially undermine the credibility of the next government, given that budget 
targets were  not met in three out of four years between 2023 and 2026, a situation that would be costly amid a 
sharp rise in long-term interest rates. Furthermore, a higher deficit would increase the amount of debt to be issued 
within an already  tight schedule. The Agence France Trésor (AFT) has announced debt issuances totaling EUR 
340 billion in 2027 (an  increase  of  EUR  20  billion  compared  to  2026).  This  increase  is  due  to  the  maturing  
COVID  related  debt (beginning in 2027 and continuing largely through 2030), while the amount of debt to be 
issued to finance the deficit will remain stable compared to 2026. A decrease from this last driver would be needed 
in subsequent years so that  the AFT’s debt issuance can stabilise.

Stéphane Colliac 
Head of the Advanced Economies Team, France 
stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

## Document 299: Irrational Exuberance In S&P 500 FEMO_

Source PDF: all report pdf/Irrational Exuberance In S&P 500 FEMO_.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: Irrational Exuberance In S&P 500 FEMO?
Date:    6 hours ago
Irrational Exuberance In S&P 500 FEMO?

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
Irrational Exuberance In S&P
500 FEMO?
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 21 Oct 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Chris Geirman / Unsplash
Are analysts' earnings expectations for the S&P 500 irrationally
exuberant? So far this year, FEMO (fabulous earnings momentum, as
measured by actual earnings per share) has been soaring, while FOMO
(fear of missing out, as reflected in P/E multiples) has been mostly
retreating. Much of that earnings optimism is concentrated in the S&P 500
Information Technology sector, particularly the Semiconductors industry,

### Page 3

where the AI investment boom has fueled extraordinary demand, pricing
power, and profit margins. But can these conditions persist? Let's take a
closer look:
(1) FEMO overwhelming FOMO. The S&P 500 is up 13.3% ytd, despite a
13.5% contraction in its forward P/E. That's because FEMO has
overwhelmed waning FOMO, with forward earnings jumping 31.0% since
the start of the year (chart).
The divergence is even more striking in Information Technology. The

sector's forward earnings has soared 65.5% ytd, while its forward P/E has
plunged 25.9%. The S&P 500 IT stock price index has risen 22.6% ytd,
powered by FEMO (chart).

### Page 4

Industry analysts remain remarkably bullish on the S&P 500's
fundamentals. Forward revenues is up 15.7% y/y, while forward earnings
is up 39.0% (chart).
(2) Semiconductors driving FEMO. Much of that optimism originates in
the IT sector, where forward revenues and earnings are up 57.2% and
91.4% y/y, respectively (chart).
Digging deeper reveals that the sector's extraordinary FEMO originates
largely in the Semiconductors industry, where forward revenues and

earnings are up 121.7% and 176.0% y/y, respectively (chart).

### Page 5

Indeed, semiconductors now account for a record 55.0% of the IT sector's
forward earnings and 40.9% of its market capitalization (chart).
The growing importance of semiconductors extends to the broader
market. Semiconductors now account for a record 20.4% of the S&P 500’s
forward earnings and 15.8% of its market capitalization (chart).

### Page 6

That makes the industry's earnings trajectory increasingly important for
the entire index. Analysts expect Semiconductor revenues and earnings to
grow 61.3% and 74.0% over the next year, respectively. More remarkably,
they expect earnings growth to average 66.1% annually over the next five
years.
These extraordinary expectations help explain why analysts project IT
sector earnings growth of 44.8% over the next year and 42.0% annually
over the next five years (chart).

In turn, analysts expect S&P 500 earnings to grow 19.0% over the next
year and an average of 26.1% annually over the next five years, the latter
approaching a record high.

### Page 7

(3) Pricing power fueling Semiconductors' FEMO. To determine
whether S&P 500 FEMO shows irrational exuberance, we need to assess
whether Semiconductor FEMO does so. Much of the industry's
extraordinary earnings growth reflects unprecedented profit margin
expansion. The industry’s forward profit margin nearly doubled from the
2023 lows to a record 51.8% during the week of October 20 (chart).
The explanation is straightforward: Demand for chips has surged as

hyperscalers and neocloud providers have raced to build AI infrastructure,
while supply is capacity constrained. The resulting pricing pressures are
clearly evident in the PPI for electronic components & accessories (up
26.8% y/y through September) and the import price index for computer &
electronic product manufacturing (up 12.4% y/y through September)
(chart).

### Page 8

(4) Will Semiconductors' pricing power persist? That's the critical
question for assessing whether Semiconductor FEMO is irrationally
exuberant. Investors already seem skeptical. The Semiconductor
industry's forward P/E has plunged to just 14.2 despite surging earnings
expectations (chart). In other words, unlike the tech bubble of 1999, any
irrational exuberance in FEMO has been mostly offset by depressed
FOMO!
The goods news is that demand for chips should remain strong. The nine

largest cloud service providers are expected to spend $957 billion on
capex this year, up 106% y/y, and more than $1.5 trillion in 2027. But
supply is starting to catch up. Global semiconductor manufacturing
capacity is expected to expand 5% this year and another 6% in 2027,
potentially easing shortages and price pressures.
(5) Irrational earnings exuberance? We suspect there is some irrational
exuberance in Semiconductor FEMO, particularly in analysts' consensus

long-term earnings growth expectations. Today's demand-supply
imbalance supports extraordinary profits, but sustaining current pricing
power and margins over a longer time horizon seems optimistic as
capacity expands. With Semiconductors accounting for more than one-
fifth of S&P 500 forward earnings, that exuberance is reflected in the
broader index.
Nevertheless, FEMO remains robust beyond Semiconductors. Analysts
expect Q3 earnings growth of 30.7% y/y, with positive growth across all 11

sectors (chart).

### Page 9

Our diffusion indexes also show that an increasing share of S&P 500
companies is experiencing rising forward revenues and earnings (chart).
Combined with expectations that semiconductor demand will continue to
outpace supply, at least over the near term, this suggests that FEMO
remains fundamentally rational.
💡
Join the discussion with Dr. Ed below! Paid members can now
leave comments or questions at the end of our QuickTakes, and it
may be featured in the next "Just Ask Dr. Ed!"

### Page 10

clause.

### Page 2

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
August inflation came in soft...
Inflation moderated in August following July’s strong 
outcome. The CPI rose 0.4% in the month, in line with our 
expectation but slightly below market consensus of 0.5%, 
taking the annual rate to 4.0%. On a seasonally adjusted 
basis, the CPI increased 0.7%mth, underscoring the impact of 
seasonal discounting in August and the start of the financial 
year repricing in July.
More closely watched are the measures of underlying inflation 
including the Trimmed Mean (TM) and market services. 
The TM rose 0.2% in August, in line with our expectations 
but again softer than the market, leaving the annual pace 
unchanged at 3.6% for a third consecutive month. Meanwhile, 
market services excluding volatiles & holiday travel increased 
0.2%mth/4.1%yr. Notable strength (three-month annualised, 
seasonally adjusted) was seen in sports participation (10.9%), 
clothing repair (7.0%), restaurant meals (6.8%) and other 
household services (5.2%). For full analysis of the August CPI, 
see “August CPI – Came in on our expectations ”.
The expenditure-weighted share of the CPI basket 
experiencing positive price shocks eased to 11% in August 
from 15% in July, while the share experiencing negative price 
shocks declined from 17% to just below 13%. The majority of 
the positive shocks were concentrated in consumer durables 
On a three-month moving average basis, the net momentum 
share (positive minus negative) rose to –4.5% from –7.7% 
in July and a trough of –15.8% in May, suggesting the 
disinflationary impulse may be nearing exhaustion. 
... with September due for a moderate pick-up
Looking ahead to September, early indicators painted a 
mixed picture. The NAB business survey showed retail price 
growth slowing to 0.9%qtr from 1.1%qtr in August, with 
labour cost growth easing to 1.7%qtr from 1.8%qtr, while 
purchase cost growth edged up to 2.4%qtr from 2.3%qtr. 
Nevertheless, all measures remain well above their long-run 
averages while the gap between purchase costs and final 
prices continues to widen, indicating businesses are facing 
margin compression (see here for further detail). Meanwhile, 
consumers’ year-ahead inflation expectations held steady 
at 4.9% in September (easing slightly at 2dp) but surged to 
5.3% in October likely reflecting higher fuel prices, with retail 
petrol and diesel averaging $2.20/l (+8.2%mth) and $2.68/l 
(+7.7%mth). We do believe the auto fuel prices are likely to see 
a near term peak in October.  Meanwhile, global shipping costs 
have also surged recently amid renewed Middle East tensions.
Sep quarter unrevised but Dec upgraded significantly
Justin Smirk, Senior Economist
Neha Sharma, Economist

3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The September CPI is expected to rise 0.6% (0.62% to 2dp)
with the annual pace lifting to 4.1%yr. Our seasonally adjusted
estimate increases 0.5% (0.48% to 2dp). For the monthly
TM, we expect a 0.3% (0.26% to 2dp) lift, holding the annual

pace steady at 3.6%. On a 3m/3m basis, the TM is tracking
a 1.0% gain in September (noting that while this measure is
conceptually different to the quarterly TM it still serves as a
useful guide). For market services excluding volatiles & holiday
travel we have pencilled in another 0.2% rise, slowing the
annual pace slightly to 4.0% (a nine-month low).
The largest contributors are recreation & culture (+0.27ppt),
driven wholly by holiday travel & accommodation. Transport

(+0.23ppt) makes a solid contribution, with gains in fuel
(+0.26ppt) partly offset by motor vehicles (–0.02ppt). Housing
also contributes (+0.13ppt), with rents and new dwellings
each adding 0.02ppt while property rates add 0.08ppt.
The key detractors in the month are food & non-alcoholic
beverages (–0.08ppt) and garments (–0.04ppt).
Key details for the month of September
Food & non-alcoholic beverages are expected to fall
0.5%mth, easing the annual pace to 2.8%yr. The weakness is

concentrated in a 3.7%mth decline in fruit & vegetables, while
non-alcoholic beverages are also expected to fall 1.5%mth.
Meals out & takeaway is forecast to rise 0.1%mth, easing the
annual rate to 3.9%.
Housing is expected to rise 0.6% in September, slowing the
annual pace to 5.5%yr. Component-wise:
• Rents are expected to rise 0.3%mth and 3.7%yr. CPI rent
inflation is nearing its peak, consistent with the moderation
in asking rent growth. We expect rent inflation to ease to

3.6%yr by end-2026 and 2.5%yr by end-2027.
• New dwelling prices, which capture only the building
component of construction costs, are expected to rise
0.2%mth and 4.9%yr. Ongoing weakness in established
housing markets would be increasing builders’ difficulty in
passing through higher costs to new builds. As a result, we
expect new dwelling inflation to moderate to 4.5%yr by
end-2026 and 2.1%yr by end-2027.
Transport inflation is expected to rise 2.0%mth in September,

lifting the annual pace to 7.7%yr reflecting an 8.3%mth
increase in fuel prices following higher oil prices through the
month. Motor vehicle prices and spare parts are expected to
fall 0.7%mth and 1.3%mth, respectively. In NSW, a one-year
vehicle registration discount between $80-100 came into
effect from 1 September, which is likely to weigh on the other
services in respect of motor vehicles component.
Recreation & culture is expected to rise 2.1%mth driven by a

4.4%mth increase in holiday travel, partly offset by a 0.4%
decline in audio, visual & computing equipment & services and
a 1.3% fall in sporting services.

### Page 4

Past performance is not a reliable indicator of future performance. The forecasts given above are predictive in character. Whilst every effort has been taken to ensure that the assumptions on which the forecasts 
are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
4
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Risks to September baseline
We view the risks around the September CPI outcome as broadly 
balanced. Waning spending momentum outside of fuel may make 
it difficult for businesses to pass through higher costs in the near 
term. However, some of these pricing pressures may be deferred 
rather than forgone. The October ban on card surcharging may 
prompt a greater-than-usual degree of price resetting, allowing 
businesses to recover some of the margin pressures that have built 
up in recent months.
Risks to housing are mixed. Trade materials suppliers continued 
to announce above-average price increases through September 
(around 8%). While soft conditions in the established housing 
market may limit the extent to which these costs are passed 
through to new dwelling prices, they nevertheless present an 
upside risk to housing-related inflation, particularly maintenance 
& repair and insurance costs. Growth in advertised rents also 
continues to ease but these new leases take some time to flow 
through to average rents paid measured in the CPI. 
A kick up in October ...
We have a solid preliminary estimate for the October CPI of 
0.6%mth lifting the annual pace to 4.8%yr. It is true that the key 
driver is an 8.9% increase in auto fuel but we estimate the TM to 
lift 0.4%mth/3.7%yr highlighting broader inflationary pressures. 
We expect that the removal of credit charge surcharges and 
resulting increase in retail prices, at most, will contribute 0.1ppt 
but only just so see it as more of a rounding error. Nevertheless, 
these changes come at a time of a significant upstream price 
pressure, particularly for SMEs, so there is some risk of broader 
price adjustments. As such, we expect marginally larger than 
usual price adjustments in October particularly in hospitality 
and household services. Being the first month of the quarter the 
October lift sets a higher base for the quarterly average estimates 
resulting in an upgrade to our December quarter estimate.
We have included a table with a monthly profile for the CPI, 
seasonally adjusted CPI and the TM out to June 2027. At 
June 2027 we have an annual pace of 3.7%yr for the CPI and 
seasonally adjusted CPI and 3.3%yr for the TM. There is also a 
table breaking down the monthly components of the CPI through 
the December quarter. 
... highlights stronger inflationary pressure ...
Our September quarter CPI estimate is unrevised at 1.3%qtr 
with the annual pace steady at 3.9%yr. At the margin there 
was a 0.04ppt increase due to a lift in auto fuel but this is 
1.29%qtr at two decimal places so still rounds to 1.3%. 
The seasonally adjusted CPI gains 1.1% in the September 
quarter highlighting that part of the increase is due to usual 
seasonality. Our TM estimate for Q3 has a small downward 
revision of 0.03ppt to 0.88% at two decimal place so our 
published estimate is also unrevised at 0.9%qtr. 
... resulting in an upward revision to Q4 2026
Westpac’s December quarter CPI estimate has been boosted 
from a 0.5%qtr to 1.3%qtr. Driving most of this change from 
our previous estimate is the 15.3% increase in autofuel, it

was 1.9%, contributing 0.55ppt to the 0.8ppt increase in the 
quarterly estimate. Outside of a 0.09ppt contribution to the 
revision from holiday travel & accommodation (revised up from 
4.2% to 5.6%) all else was via small contributions for a large 
number of expenditure categories. This is why there is a 0.2ppt 
increase in our September quarter TM estimate from 0.7%qtr 
to 0.9%qtr.
A disinflationary impulse kicks in for 2027
We now expect auto fuel prices to peak in October (240 cpl 
for petrol, 284 cpl for diesel) and then to trend lower through 
2027 (184 cpl for petrol and 194 cpl for diesel by December 
2027) leading to a 13.6% falling in auto fuel in the first half 
of 2027 subtracting 0.46ppt off the CPI over the same period 
of time. For a detailed description on how we constructed the 
auto fuels profile please see: “ Persistent energy pressures 
have further to run ” 
The first half of 2027 will also see an almost 5% decline in 
holiday travel & accommodation which will subtract a further 
0.29ppt off the CPI. However, outside of that any broader 
moderation in inflation is expected to be more muted hence 
we have the TM printing 0.7%qtr for each quarter in 2027.  
Critical to this moderation in core inflation is our expect step 
down in dwelling price inflation on the back of falling estab -
lished home prices. From a 1.1%qtr increase in Q3 2026 we 
see dwelling price increasing by 0.7% in the Q4 2026, and 
then 0.6% in both Q1 and Q2 2027 before dropping to 0.5%qtr 
in Q3 and Q4 2027. This will see dwelling price inflation in the 
CPI drop from 5.4%yr at June 2026 to 3.0%yr by June 2027 
then down to 2.2%yr by December 2027. 
At least for the near term a key uncertainty remains the extent 
to which businesses are able to pass through ongoing labour 
and non-labour cost pressures and how this unwinds through 
2027 as energy prices ease and the domestic economy slows 
under the weight of higher interest rates and falling estab -
lished home prices. There is a risk that firms face greater 
resistance to price increases if discretionary household spend -
ing softens by more than expected. That said, these headwinds 
may be at least partially offset by the stronger starting point 
for household incomes, which has proven more resilient than 
previously anticipated, and by higher energy prices due to the 
ongoing conflict in the Middle East.
That said, there is also the risk that the disinflationary pulse by 
the second half of 2027 could be greater than what we antici-

pate. Another rate hike in November, along with a deeper than

and thus a more significant moderation in price setting behav -
iour. It is also possible that our auto fuel prices fall by more 
than we anticipate. For while we have spot crude prices to fall 
to about pre-crisis levels by end 2027, a combination of a slow 
recovery in refining capacity, constrained return of physical 
supplies and higher shipping cost keep Australian fuel prices 
above their pre-conflict level. Should all these factors improve 
fast than we expect, and auto fuel prices return to pre-conflict 
level if not lower, then headline inflation could dip below 2%yr 
and the TM would be closer to 2.5%yr (the mid point if the 
RBA’s inflation target), by end 2027.

### Page 2

The Week Ahead October 9, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         2

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

Monetary Authority of Singapore (MAS)

We expect another slight increase in the 
SGD NEER policy band slope at the MAS’ 
October review, with no change to the 
band’s width or centre. Import cost 
pressures and resilient growth favour a 
calibrated tightening, rather than a more 
forceful adjustment, in line with the 
global monetary policy stance.

Also due to be released alongside the 
MAS’ policy decision are the 3Q26 
advance GDP estimates, which we 
expect to show another quarter of strong 
growth at 5.4% yoy (1.4% qoq sa), 
compared with 5.9% yoy (1.4% qoq sa) in 
2Q26. Robust economic performance 
continued to be driven by artificial 
intelligence ( AI)-led trade activity, 
supporting the manufacturing and 
wholesale trade sectors, while strength 
in the financial sector underpinned the 
resilience of the modern services cluster. 
This came despite a moderation of the 
transport & storage sector, and 
weakness in consumer -facing food 
services and retail sectors.

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

Malaysia

We expect Malaysia’s advance GDP 
estimate for 3Q26 to register above -
trend growth of 5.0% yoy, albeit 
moderating from 6.0% yoy  in 2Q26. The 
overall expansion continued to be 
supported by ongoing strength in the 
export-oriented manufacturing sector, 
underpinned by strong  AI-related 
demand for electronics, and sustained 
domestic demand, backed by healthy 
investment and private consumption 
trends. However, contraction in mining 
and weak agricultural activity dragged 
overall growth.

Singapore

We expect firm Singapore non -oil 
domestic exports (NODX) growth of 28.0% 
yoy in September 2026, although 
moderating from August’s exceptional 
surge of 46.2%. Although favourable 
base effects largely faded in September, 
electronics shipments likely remained 
the primary growth driver, underpinned 
by robust AI -related demand amid 
sustained global investment in AI 
infrastructure, while non-electronics 
exports were uneven.

### Page 3

The Week Ahead October 9, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         3

China

Exports growth is expected to remain 
robust at 25.7% y oy in September, 
supported by continued strength in 
electronics demand. Both the official and 
Caixin manufacturing PMIs rose above 
the 50 expansion threshold during the 
month, pointing to improving 
manufacturing activity. External trade 
indicators also stren gthened, with 
average daily deadweight tonnage 
handled at China's 20 major ports 
accelerating from -0.7% yoy in August to 
7.2% in September. Freight activity 
improved as well, as growth in 
international cargo flights picked up 
from -1.4% y oy to 3.6% over the same 
period.

On the price front, factory -gate and raw 
material prices rebounded amid the 
renewed escalation in geopolitical 
tensions. Main raw material purchasing 
price and producer price subPMI 
increased from 56.6 and 50.4 to 60.8 and 
54.0 during the period. However, 
downstream consumer price pressures 
are likely to remain relatively subdued 
under the pricing-band mechanism. As a 
result, CPI inflation is expected to edge 
up from 0.8% y oy in August to 1.0% in 
September. Nevertheless, soft domestic 
demand is expected to continue 
constraining the pace of consumer price 
increases.

India

September inflation is likely to gather 
momentum to 5.7% y/y, partly driven by 
base effects, from 4.8% the month 
before. Food segments continue to 
register gains, with pressures 
broadening to include perishables, 
edible oils, rice, pulses, sugar, etc., 
according to high-frequency data. Under 
non-food, retail pump prices have not 
been adjusted further, though other fuel 
products, such as non -subsidised LPG, 
diesel and ATF (still up by double digits 
y/y, though slower than in August), are 
up on the year. Core  inflation is also 
likely to tick up, aligning with the central 
bank's view that price risks are no longer 
benign. Pressures have built up due to 
the overhang of an uneven and sub -par 
monsoon, drought conditions in parts of 
the country, and elevated oil pr ices. In 
the face of higher headline prints, we 
expect the RBI to keep the door open for 
another rate hike in December 2026.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS  
NOVEMBER CPI
PREVIEW AND ANALYSIS
Complete Montly CPI will take some time to understand
19 December 2025
Key points
• The October monthly CPI was 
flat in the month, broadly as we 
expected but stronger than market 
expectations. For more information see 
“Electrical noise is far from static”.
• The impact on our inflation profile 
was marginal. The December quarter 
CPI remains 0.6%qtr/3.6%yr, while 
the Trimmed Mean edged up from 
0.7%qtr/3.1%yr to 0.8%qtr/3.2%yr.
• Our November monthly CPI estimate 
is 0.4% for the month, leaving the 
annual pace unchanged at 3.8%yr.
• The introduction of the complete 
monthly CPI is a significant enhancement 
to our analysis of price formation in 
the Australian economy. However, 
with many series having only a short 
history, it will take time to fully 
understand some of the finer details.
• For now, we will continue to place 
greater emphasis on the quarterly 
measures of core inflation.

### Page 2

Past performance is not a reliable indicator of future performance. The forecasts given above are predictive in character. Whilst every effort has been taken to ensure that the assumptions on which the forecasts 
are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
No surprise in the October CPI
The new complete monthly CPI gained 3.8% in the year to 
October, coming in a touch softer than Westpac’s estimate of 
3.9%yr but a bit stronger than the market estimate of 3.6%yr. 
So while the market and other commentators may have been 
surprised by the result it was broadly within our expectations 
and as such has had just a marginal impact on our inflation 
profile.
The October CPI was flat in the month, a touch softer than 
Westpac’s published near-cast of 0.1% on the back of a larger 
than expected decline in electricity (–10.2%) which was only 
partially offset by a smaller than expected decline in auto fuel 
(–0.9%) and an unexpected bump up in garments (+2.6%).
The Trimmed Mean (TM) lifted 0.3% in the month of October, 
the same monthly increase it has seen for the previous two 
months and down from the 0.5%mth increase in July but 
stronger than the 0.2%mth prints from March to June.
This saw the annual pace of the TM increase to 3.3% from 
3.2% in September. This month the TM excluded the annual 
rise in electricity; alongside other large price rises and falls. 
Due to the short history of many series in the monthly CPI, the 
annual pace of monthly TM inflation can only be calculated 
back to April 2025. Before then the ABS notes that the annual 
pace is calculated from the quarterly CPI data.
For more information on the October monthly CPI see 
“Electrical noise is far from static ”.
Monthly CPI points to some momentum
Following the October CPI we have seen some minor revisions 
to the various components in our December quarter CPI 
estimate with the upward revisions, such as transport via auto 
fuel and motor vehicles, being offset by downward revisions 
to recreation, alcohol & tobacco and utilities in particular. This 
has left our December CPI estimate unchanged at 0.6%qtr.   
As our Chief Economist, Luci Ellis, has noted that inflation 
did see a bump in the September quarter and again in the 
month of October. However, the main sources of the surprise 
have had little to do with domestic demand or labour market 
pressures. Rather, a sizeable part of the bump looks to have 
been from administered prices and noise. Since the release of 
the data, the RBA has recognised this issue but at the post 
RBA Board Decision Media Conference in December, the 
Governor communicated that the Bank is more worried about 
upside risks to inflation than first thought.
We agree that some of this momentum will continue into the 
December quarter as highlighted by our upward revision in our 
We will continue to focus on the quarterly prints
Justin Smirk
Senior Economist

### Page 3

Past performance is not a reliable indicator of future performance. The forecasts given above are predictive in character. Whilst every effort has been taken to ensure that the assumptions on which the forecasts 
are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
December quarter TM estimate to 0.8%qtr/3.2%yr from our 
earlier estimate of 0.7%qtr/3.1%yr. 
For the December quarter, price changes that are included in 
the TM go from a low of –0.26% to a high of 1.66%. Readers 
will remember that the TM is calculated from seasonally 
adjusted price changes and not the original estimates so if 
unaffected by regular seasonal price shifts. We estimate 
that the most significant components to be trimmed off 
the top in the quarter are: electricity (2.7%); other services 
for motor vehicles (2.5%); tobacco (2.1%); and automotive 
fuel (1.7%). Trimmed off the bottom are: other non-durable 
household products (–0.39%); garments for women (–0.92%); 
international holiday travel (–0.97%); vegetables (–1.16%) and 
wine (–1.27%).   
However, we do not expect this momentum to continue 
through 2026. As Luci noted in her article “ RBA on hold 
in 2026, risks on both sides ” we expect inflation to keep 
easing, due to the lagged impact of past restrictive policy 
and the unwind of earlier government support measures. We 
believe that many commentators have overlooked these lags. 
Monetary policy typically takes a year or more to fully affect 
jobs and prices, so current outcomes reflect the peak cash rate 
from a year ago and we expect to see further labour market 
softening. Strong hiring across the care economy has masked 
some of that restrictiveness, as has public infrastructure 
spending, but these supports are now fading along with last 
year’s tax-cut boost. We believe that even with the 75bps in  
rate cuts this year, policy remains mildly restrictive, and given 
usual lags, this will keep showing in the data through 2026.
Taking a look at some of the recent larger moves in monthly 
prices can highlight many of the issues Luci raises. Taking the 
seasonally adjusted data, smoothing it with a three month 
average then calculating the change over three months (so we 
have three months on three months percentage change) we 
find at the top end of the list some price increases you would 
not expect to continue at the same pace. 
For example at the top are: lamb & goat (8.1%, highly 
seasonal); accessories (8.1% with jewellery prices surging 
on the back of record high gold prices); audio visual & 
computing services (6.0% due to Netflix subscriptions); beef 
& veal (3.7%, highly seasonal); coffee & tea (3.6% noting 
global coffee prices have passed their peak and are already 
significantly lower); water & sewerage (2.3% IPART decision 
was for a 13.8% increase for Sydney Water in October); 
property rates & charges (2.3% administered prices); and 
electricity (1.2%, reflecting unwinding of cost of living 
rebates). Offsetting some of these increases was falling prices 
for: men’s footwear (–2.0%); vegetables (–2.0% seasonal); 
eggs (–1.5%, reflecting the unwinding of the price spike 
associated with an outbreak of bird flu); and major household 
appliances (–1.2%). 
Core inflation still to moderate through 2026
We are projecting headline inflation to get back to the RBA’s 
target band, but not until later in 2026. We now see core 
inflation back below the midpoint of the 2–3% range at the 
end of the year. We had thought this might be achieved by the 
third quarter but now expect current momentum to continue

  of which, holiday travel 1.1 2.0 3.0 1.6
Education 5.4 5.4 5.4 5.4
